## Document 047: 2026年9月14日國際經濟金融週報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月14日國際經濟金融週報.pdf
Report source: Mega Bank (國際經濟金融週報)
Date: 2026-09-14
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
中華民國一一五年九月十四日

國際經濟金融週報

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國財政壓力仍高，房市降溫與通膨回升增添經濟不確定性 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1,668 億美元，年減 51.6%，主因收入增
加、支出下降，但部分支出提前至7 月支付，影響單月表現。累計前11
個月赤字仍近 2 兆美元，關稅收入轉正，惟利息支出持續攀升，財政壓
力仍高。 
⚫ 8 月美國成屋銷售年率降至 398 萬戶，月減 2.0%、年減 1.2%，為 2025
年 6 月以來首度跌破 400 萬戶，顯示高房貸利率持續抑制房市需求。房
價中位數為 42.91 萬美元，年增 1.6%，連續 38 個月上漲；待售成屋庫
存增至 162 萬戶，相當於 4.9 個月供給量，創逾 10 年高點。整體而言，
成屋市場呈現銷售放緩、房價續升與庫存增加態勢，供給改善有助市場
逐步趨於平衡。 
⚫ 8 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%，高於上月 0.1%，年增率維持
3.4%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，年增率則降至 2.4%。能源價格由跌轉升，
其中汽油上漲 3.9%， 住房月增0.3%， 為推升通膨主因 ； 食品月增0.1%。
整體而言，短期物價壓力升溫，但核心通膨仍持續降溫。 
⚫ 8 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)月增 0.4%、年增 5.4%，高於上月的 0.1%
及 4.8%，主要反映能源價格回升影響，其中柴油價格大漲24.1%；服務
價格則受運輸及倉儲成本增加推升而續呈上揚。核心 PPI 月增 0.3%，
低於前月的 0.4%，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍具韌性，惟基礎通膨動能尚
未明顯升溫。 
重要 
事件

伸至市場准入領域，增添北美供應鏈及貿易前景不確定性。

一、 美國—財政壓力仍高，房市降溫與通膨回升增添經濟不確定性 
2026 年 8 月(2026 財年第 11 個月)美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1,668.0 億美元，
較去年同期 3,447.9 億美元縮減 51.6%；當月政府收入約 3,600.3 億美元，年增
4.6%，政府支出約 5,268.3 億美元，年減23.5%，惟部分原定 8 月初支付的軍人薪
資與退休金、退伍軍人福利、補充保障收入及部分醫療保險給付款項因 8 月 1 日
適逢非工作日而提前至 7 月支付，使單月支出受到付款時點影響。 收入方面，8
月關稅淨收入約 128.4 億美元，扭轉 7 月關稅淨收入為負 85.5 億美元的情況；累
計前 11 個月關稅淨收入約 1,673.1 億美元，較去年同期增加 1.3%。 支出方面，
8 月淨利息支出約 856.1 億美元，累計前 11 個月達約 1 兆 169.7 億美元，年增
8.9%，顯示利息負擔持續增加。 累計 2026 財年前 11 個月，美國聯邦政府財政
赤字約 1 兆 9,655.9 億美元，較去年同期小幅縮減 0.4%，其中收入年增 3.3%至約
4 兆 8,454.5 億美元，支出年增 2.2%至約 6 兆 8,110.4 億美元。整體而言，8 月單
月赤字雖明顯縮減 ， 但部分受到付款時點移轉影響 ， 且累計赤字仍接近2 兆美元 ，
加上利息支出持續攀升，美國聯邦財政壓力仍維持高檔。 
2026 年 8 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率降至 398 萬戶 ， 較上月減少2.0%，
較去年同期下降 1.2%，並為 2025 年 6 月以來首次跌破 400 萬戶，顯示高房貸利
率持續抑制房市交易動能。區域表現方面，西部銷售持平，東北部、中西部及南
部分別月減 4.0%、3.1%及 1.6%；房價方面，成屋售價中位數為42.91 萬美元，年
增 1.6%，連續 38 個月上漲。供給端則持續改善，待售成屋庫存增至 162 萬戶，
月增 3.2%、年增 5.9%，相當於 4.9 個月供給量，為近 10 多年來最高水準，住房
可負擔指數亦由去年同期的 101.2 升至 104.7。整體而言，8 月美國成屋市場呈現
銷售放緩、房價續漲及庫存增加的格局，高利率仍抑制成交動能，惟供給增加及
可負擔性改善，有助提升購屋者的選擇與議價空間，並促進市場逐步趨於平衡。 
2026 年 8 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，高於上月的
0.1%，年增率維持 3.4%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，高於前月的 0.2%，但年增率由
2.5%降至 2.4%。在細項方面，能源價格由跌轉升，能源指數月增 2.1%，其中汽
油價格上漲 3.9%，成為推升整體物價的主要因素；住房指數月增 0.3%，食品指

數月增 0.1%。此外，通訊、外宿住宿、機票、教育及車輛價格均呈上漲，醫療照

2026 年 8 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，高於上月之
0.1%，年增率則由上月的 4.8%升至 5.4%。其中最終需求商品價格月增 1.1%，結
束前兩個月下跌走勢 ， 超過四分之三的漲幅係由能源價格月增4.2%所帶動 ； 細項
中，柴油價格大漲 24.1%，汽油、航空燃油及燃料油價格亦同步上升，食品價格
則微增 0.1%。另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.1%，已連續第三個月上升，
主要受運輸及倉儲服務價格上漲 2.3%支撐 ， 其中貨車貨運服務及航空客運服務價
格分別上漲 2.0%及 4.2%，惟批發及零售業利潤率下滑，使部分服務價格漲幅受
到抵銷。此外，剔除食品、能源及貿易服務後之核心最終需求PPI 月增 0.3%，低
於 7 月之 0.4%，年增率維持 4.7%。整體而言，8 月 PPI 回升主要反映能源價格反
彈及運輸成本上升影響，顯示美國生產端通膨壓力仍具韌性；惟核心 PPI 增幅較
前月略為放緩，基礎通膨動能尚未進一步升溫，後續仍須持續關注能源價格波動
及服務成本變化對整體物價走勢之影響。 
近期美加貿易衝突持續升溫，雙方關稅報復行動已進一步擴大至非關稅措施
領域 。 加拿大自9 月 8 日起正式對約 200 億美元以上美國商品課徵 15%至 50%不
等的報復性關稅，涵蓋鋼鐵、鋁製品、家電、服飾及農業設備等產品，以回應美
國先前對加拿大商品加徵關稅措施 。 面對加拿大反制 ， 美國政府隨即宣布擴大限
制措施，除關稅措施外，將自 9 月 29 日起限制部分加拿大乳製品、酒類及機車
產品輸美，並限制加拿大企業參與部分美國政府採購標案，使雙方爭端由關稅戰
延伸至市場准入及政府採購領域。 加拿大總理 Mark Carney 表示，美國措施可
能衝擊加拿大關鍵產業發展，加方將持續採取對等回應措施。市場亦關注美方後
續可能進一步提高加拿大汽車、鋼鐵及相關零組件關稅，增添北美供應鏈及企業
投資規劃不確定性。整體而言，美加經貿關係短期內仍難見明顯緩和，後續發展
恐進一步增添北美及全球貿易前景不確定性。 
美國國會近期推進針對俄羅斯的新制裁法案，內容包括授權總統對俄羅斯原
油或天然氣主要購買國的進口商品課徵最高 100%關稅，以進一步限制俄羅斯能
源收入並加大對俄施壓 。 該法案已獲參議院以高票通過 ， 眾議院則預計展開審議 ，

二、歐元區—歐洲央行如期升息一碼，且仍有繼續升息的空間 
 歐元區第二季經濟成長季率終值由初值上修 0.2 個百分點至 0.6%，但上修主
因來自愛爾蘭經濟大幅成長 10.2%；若排除愛爾蘭，則歐元區第二季經濟成長季
率僅約 0.3%。從需求面觀察，第二季消費貢獻 0.2 個百分點，雖然通膨上升侵蝕
購買力，但薪資漲幅仍優於預期，加上高儲蓄率支持，使消費動能得以延續。其
中 ， 服務消費維持穩健成長 ， 耐久財支出亦明顯增加 。 另方面 ， 淨出口貢獻0.9 個
百分點，但設備投資下滑使整體固定投資表現仍顯疲弱。 
 歐洲央行 9 月 10 日如期宣布升息一碼，存款機制利率調升為 2.5%，但仍維
持「依經濟數據逐次決策」的立場。值得注意的是，本次歐洲央行分別上修2026
年、2027 年經濟成長率 0.1、0.2 個百分點至 0.9%、1.4%，以及 2028 年核心通膨
上修 0.1 個百分點至 2.3%， 反映歐洲央行認為經濟尚具韌性 ， 即便通膨壓力增溫 ，
仍能減輕其負面影響，使得政策利率繼續上調的空間，故一般預期歐洲央行將會
在 10 月政策會議採取觀望，並在12 月宣布升息一碼，2027 年第一季升息機率亦
有所上升，歐洲央行貨幣政策短期內仍將維持偏鷹立場。 
三、日本—內閣府上修第二季經濟成長，薪資動能續強 
2026 年第二季經濟成長率由 1.1%上修至 1.4%，主要受國內需求及庫存
貢獻改善帶動，其中以資本支出修正為國內需求上修的主因。展望下半年，
在政府財政刺激措施、企業持續擴大設備投資及薪資成長帶動消費支出的支
撐下，日本經濟可望維持溫和成長態勢，惟外部需求復甦力道、能源價格變
動及地緣政治風險仍為影響經濟表現的不確定因素。 
7 月薪資年增 4.7%，優於市場預期，其中夏季獎金年增 6.3%為主要推升
因素；經常性薪資及兼職時薪分別年增 4.1%及 5.7%，增速均較前期擴大 ， 顯
示薪資成長動能持續增強。此外，實質薪資年增3.4%，反映勞工購買力進一
步改善。在企業獲利表現穩健支撐下，儘管仍面臨成本上升及外部環境不確
定性 ， 企業仍維持獎金發放與加薪趨勢 ， 有助支撐民間消費韌性及經濟表現 。

四、中國—進出口同步強勁成長，貿易順差增幅則相對有限 
8 月出口年增率較 7 月回升 1.1 個百分點至 25%，出口總值為 4,014 億美元；
累計前八個月出口年增率為 19.3%，累積出口總值為 29,238 億美元。前八個月
IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)累積出口額分別成長 103.9%、49.4%；船
舶累積出口成長 35.2%；汽車累積出口成長 53.2%。另方面，8 月進口年增率較 7
月回升 0.7 個百分點至 28.2%， 進口總值為2,824 億美元 ； 累計前八個月進口值成
長 28.2%至 21,183 億美元，其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)進口分別
成長 61.7%、105.1%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部分伺服器關鍵組件有賴
於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前八個月貿易順差達 8,055 億美元，僅較去年
同期增加 2.6%， 增幅明顯低於出口 ， 顯示出口擴張同時伴隨關鍵原物料進口大幅
增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價值產品依賴國際供應鏈支
持。 
8 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 7 月的 3.5%回升至 3.8%，主因原油價格自 7 月
起走升，其影響逐漸反映於上游生產成本。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格年
增率反彈 7.3 個百分點至 10.5%，並帶動燃料加工業通膨上揚，抵銷近期鋼鐵價
格下滑，導致相關礦產開採及冶煉加工產品價格較去年同期下滑的影響。此外，
對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學工業、化纖產業通膨沒有明顯變動，部分可能
與先前油價上漲期間累積較高庫存有關，惟在近期油價再度走升下，不排除相關
成本壓力於未來數月逐步反映。另因金價盤整，有色金屬礦採選業出廠價格年增
率亦持續放慢。 
8 月消費者通膨(CPI)回升 0.3 個百分點為 0.8%，但仍然是近七個月的次低水
準，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)回升 0.1 個百分點至 1 %。當月受國際原油價格上
漲影響，燃油通膨反彈 7.5 個百分點至 8.3%；手機零組件上漲，通信工具通膨上
揚 2.3 個百分點至 10.6%；夏季旅遊需求支持旅遊服務通膨增加 0.1 個百分點至
2.7%；其他商品及服務價格則大致平穩。整體而言，8 月能源價格有所回升，但
在企業庫存水位仍偏高情況下，抑制成本的傳導速度，除燃油價格外，尚未明顯
反映於其他消費項目通膨。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，若油價維持高檔或
進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。

### Page 9

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

8

五、印度及東南亞—油價上揚、通膨升溫，進口成本上漲恐使貿易收支惡化 
東南亞:(1)印尼：7 月出口額 262.2 億美元、年增 6.05%，非油氣產品出口年增
6.84%，礦物燃料、鋼鐵及電氣機械及設備出口增幅均逾 2 成，油氣產品出口
大幅下滑 14.58%；對美國（8.61%）、中國（17.52%）、日本（7.93%）、東
協（16.72%） 出口強勁成長。7 月進口年增 27.02%達 260.9 億美元，創歷史新
高，非油氣產品及油氣產品進口分別年增 23.83%、49.91%，以機械設備、電
機及電氣設備及塑膠品進口成長為主 ， 自中國、 歐盟進口成長23.49%、26.32%、
自美進口成長 13.1%。進出口相抵，7 月貿易收支意外轉為順差 1.3 億美元。
印尼經濟較依賴進口 ， 部分進口需求係配合國內投資及生產活動擴張 ，另國際
油價上漲增加能源進口負擔，在出口成長幅度相對有限情形下 ， 進口持續快速
增加將壓縮商品貿易順差，並可能進一步擴大經常帳赤字。8 月消費者物價指
數(CPI)年增率升至 3.19%，主要因食品、飲料和香菸價格上漲，以及個人照護
和交通成本增加所致 ，剔除波動較大的食品和政府管制價格的核心通膨率升至
2.92%，為 2023 年 3 月以來最高。儘管通膨有所回升，但仍維持在印尼央行
1.5%至 3.5%的目標區間內。(2)泰國：8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率上升至
2.53%，主要因中東衝突導致油價上漲，進而推高國內燃料價格所致，食品、
非食品價格通膨分別上升 2.99%、2.23%，剔除能源及生鮮食品的核心通膨率
上揚至 1.44%，創 3 年多新高，泰國商務部維持其 2026 年整體通膨預期在
1.5%-2.5%之間，通膨水準仍處於泰國央行 1%-3%的目標區間內。(3)菲律賓：
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率從 7 月的 6.2%降至 6.1%， 主要因食品/非酒精
飲料、房屋/公用事業價格漲幅放緩，惟受燃油價格上漲影響，交通運輸價格
進一步上揚 13.5%，核心通膨率小幅降至 4.1%，1 月至 8 月平均通膨率為 5.2%，
仍高於菲律賓央行 2%-4%的目標區間，在國際油價大幅上揚下，央行升息壓
力漸增。(4)越南: 8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率上升至 4.89%，創 3 個月新
高，主要是運輸成本上升所致，儘管高於越南國家銀行(SBV)4.5%目標，惟核
心通膨穩定於 4.5%， 在經濟動能仍佳下 ， 央行尚有餘裕採取審慎觀望的政策。
8 月出口額年增 26%至 548 億美元，其中電子產品出口成長 41%為主要驅動
力，機械設備成長 34%居次，對美國出口大增 24.7%；8 月進口額年增 37.9%
至 549.1 億美元，以電子產品、電腦及零件成長 80%為最，機械設備居次。進
出口相抵，8 月貿易逆差收窄至 11 億美元。整體而言，越南受惠於人工智慧
需求及中國供應鏈移轉，帶動電子產品出口，但主要集中於外資企業，資本財

### Page 10

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

9

與生產投入高度仰賴進口， 對國內生產活動增值十分有限 ，越南政府正積極推
動投資技術轉移、提升治理能力及開拓市場，加強本土企業之間的聯繫，以創
造更大的附加價值。 
五、台灣—通膨略降溫，AI 需求支撐出口成長 
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.04%，雖較前兩月回落，但已連續 4 個月高
於 2%的通膨警戒線，主要受國際油價上漲帶動油料費、機票及旅遊團費增加，
加上外食費、房租及電費等價格上漲所致；惟蔬菜價格因去年基期較高而大幅下
跌，抵銷部分漲幅。整體而言，通膨壓力雖略有降溫，但核心 CPI 仍達 2.30%，
顯示外食、房租等服務類價格仍具黏著性，物價上漲壓力尚未完全消退。 
 8 月出口達 824 億美元，年增 41%，續創歷年單月新高，主要受惠於人工智
慧應用快速擴展及國際雲端服務業者持續加大資本支出，帶動半導體、伺服器及
相關資通訊產品需求強勁。雖然部分傳統產業仍受全球景氣復甦步調不一及貿易
環境不確定性影響，但科技產品需求熱絡使整體出口表現優於預期。展望未來，
在 AI、 高效能運算及雲端服務需求持續成長 ， 以及第四季傳統外銷旺季來臨帶動
下，我國出口動能可望延續，惟仍須關注地緣政治風險及國際貿易政策變化對外
需之影響。

### Page 1

ab 5 October 2026
Global Research
Global I/O
Smartphones
August ’26 Sell-Through: No signs of recovery
yet
Global smartphone unit sell-through -8.9% YoY August '26 YTD
Global smartphone unit sell-through (source: Counterpoint) for the month of August
'26 came in at 85.9m units (-0.1% MoM, -12.3% YoY), marking the ninth consecutive
month of YoY sell-through decline (was -12.2%/-10.9% YoY in July/June). By region, all
regions except for the US (-3.4% YoY) saw double-digit YoY declines, with India seeing

the most incremental downside at -17.7% YoY, followed by China (-17.3%), Europe (-
10.7%) and RoW (-10.1%). Following eight months of significant YoY weakness,
August '26 YTD global smartphone unit sell-through now sits at -8.9% YoY with all
regions well in negative comps (India/China/Europe/RoW/US: -12.3%/-10.5%/-8.7%/-
8.0%/-4.4% YoY).
Apple outperformance continues, Samsung beginning to rebound
Apple: YTD iPhone unit sell-through (149.3m units, +8.7% YoY) continues to highlight

the material outperformance of Apple, which remains the only major OEM (>10% unit
market share) in positive comps. With key iPhone suppliers optimistic on unit growth in
'27 (link) on the back of (1) the change in launch schedule (3 models each in 1Q27 and
3Q27) and (2) the introduction of iPhone Duo (~10m units in first 12 months), we
continue to forecast Apple strength to continue beyond '26 (UBSe sell-in: +8.5% YoY)
and into '27 (+4.7% YoY). Samsung: August '26 sell-through came in at 20.3m units

(+0.9% YoY), returning to positive comps for the first time since March '26, primarily on
the back of the release of the new flagship foldable models (Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Fold8,
Flip8) released on August 7th. New foldable sales in August came in at 2.8m units, only
0.3m units (or 7.5%) below July+August '25 sales for last year's lineup (Galaxy Z Fold7,
Flip7, Flip7 FE; released on July 25 th, 2025) despite two less weeks of sales. Whilst
YTD sell-through still remains soft at -4.1% YoY, we believe resilient sales of the new

flagship model as well as incremental share gains in the mid-range should help Samsung
achieve YoY unit growth (UBSe '26E sell-in: +4.4% YoY). China Android: We continue
to see memory-driven downside materialising with Transsion, Oppo, Vivo, and Xiaomi
YoY sell-through decline now at -23.1%/-19.0%/-18.2%/-18.1%.
Reiterating our sell-in forecasts of -10.0%/-3.0% YoY in '26E/'27E
We entered the year with a cautious view on the smartphone industry (link) primarily on

the back of rapidly rising memory costs. We also flagged that these dynamics will be
much more penalising for Chinese OEMs focusing on the low-end/mid-range segment.
With the DRAM/NAND upcycles likely to extend until at least 2Q28E/4Q27E and unit
sell-through now down 8.9% YoY globally (and on average 16.0% for Transsion, Oppo,
Vivo, and Xiaomi) eight months into the year, we expect to see double-digit YoY unit
decline % and reiterate our '26E sell-in forecast of -10.0% YoY. Heading into '27, with

memory likely to remain an incremental headwind, we expect to see muted demand for
smartphones and see units declining further from a low base. We recently lowered our
YoY unit sell-in forecasts for '27E to -3.0% from +2.0% (link).
Selective on smartphone plays; prefer memory/MLCC suppliers
Within the smartphone supply chain, we continue to prefer MediaTek and Samsung
(both APAC Key Call Buy) as well as ASE, Hon Hai, JCET, Micron, Murata, SEMCO, SK

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
The handset industry, as part of consumer electronics, is cyclically sensitive to the economy
and so upside and downside risks relate to macro drivers. Other downside risks include the
rapid loss of market share due to poor product timing, design or distribution. The supply
chain companies into the handset industry also operate in a highly cyclical market both from
macro concerns and inventory cycles within the supply chain. These companies also operate

in a highly competitive environment with product innovation and R&D investments critical to
success. Valuation methods across the sector include multiple analysis.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Accton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
September Sales -1% MoM/
+66% YoY
In this report, we focus on Accton's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for share price movement.
Details:
• September sales were NT$40,399mn (-1% MoM/+66% YoY).
• 3Q26 revenue was NT$120,744mn (+26% QoQ/+66% YoY), in line with MSe
of NT$120,883mn (+27% QoQ/+66% YoY)  and consensus of NT$117.390mn
(+23% QoQ/+61% YoY) by 3%.
Our view:
• We believe the consignment arrangement of DRAM, PCB, etc., from August

might have capped the upside on revenue growth, but it should be a
favorable factor for gross margin, emerging from August/September and
likely extending into 4Q26.
• Driven by the network switch business and its major customer's AI
accelerator module ramp, we expect revenue to continue growing
sequentially in 4Q26, with the momentum in 2027 strengthened by potential
new customers and new products.
• Stay OW with a PT of NT$3,500, based on 30x 2027e P/E.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Accton Technology Corporation (2345.TW)
Base case, P/E, which we believe better captures the company's earnings growth momentum.
We assign a target P/E of 30x to our 2027 EPS estimate, which we view as justified by an
operating profit CAGR of 48% for 2025-28, based on our estimates. This implies a PEG of
around 0.6, vs. its average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for
an operating profit CAGR of 32%.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger data center capex
n Faster network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand losing share in AI networks
n Stronger custom AI accelerator growth
n Stronger ODM direct business model adoption
Risks to Downside
n Weaker data center capex
n Slower network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
n Softer custom AI accelerator growth
n Softer ODM direct business model adoption

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

按美元计，9月我国进口同比增长31%，前值增28.2%；出口同比增长36.3%，前值增25%。9月份贸易顺差
1335.32亿美元，前值1190.85亿美元。9月进出口同比增速较上月有所抬升，继续保持较快增长。出口方面，
产品结构持续优化。从主要出口市场看，9月我国前三大出口市场分别是东南亚联盟、美国和欧盟，其中，
对东盟出口同比增长52.26%，对欧盟出口增长39.56%，对美国出口增长25.25%，对美国、欧盟出口大幅改

善，对东盟及“一带一路”沿线国家出口保持高速增长。从产品结构看，出口产品持续向智向绿方向转型，
机电产品特别是集成电路同比增长显著。进口方面，随着高质量共建“一带一路”扎实推进，我国与全球
贸易伙伴的经贸合作持续深化，优质产品持续进入我国市场。从进口产品结构看，金属矿砂等大宗商品进
口同比增速持续提升；自动数据处理设备及其零部件等机电产品同比增长显著，或能说明高新产业的高出
口需求支撑了进口的景气。展望未来，近期积极因素有所积累，但美元强势带来的去杠杆效应能否缓解，
仍取决于地缘局势能否明朗。具体来看，出口方面重点关注可能增加的贸易壁垒。进口方面，经济政策的
回归或仍需和外部环境相协调，关注后续国内相应政策的落地。

## Document 119: 9月金融数据点评：关注外部环境对适度宽松货币政策的影响

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月金融数据点评：关注外部环境对适度宽松货币政策的影响.pdf
Report source: 中银证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济 | 证券研究报告 — 总量点评 2026 年 10 月 15 日

相关研究报告 
《上下游物价指标分歧加大》20261014 
《9 月通胀点评》20261014 
《前三季度进出口数据点评》20261014

中银国际证券股份有限公司 
具备证券投资咨询业务资格 
宏观经济

证券分析师：张晓娇 
xiaojiao.zhang@bocichina.com 
证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300514010002

证券分析师：朱启兵 
(8610)66229359 
Qibing.Zhu@bocichina.com 
证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

9 月金融数据点评

关注外部环境对适度宽松货币政策的影响   
9 月新增社融和新增信贷均好于万得一致预期；企业融资需求分层，信用度
较高的企业以企业债为代表的融资需求下降，依赖贷款渠道的企业贷款 9 月
表现相对更好；居民部门加杠杆意愿不强；受资本市场波动影响，存款搬家
有变化的趋势；关注海外加息周期和高利率环境对国内金融条件和货币政策
的潜在影响。 
 9 月新增社融 3.55 万亿元，较去年同期多增 192 亿元，较 8 月多增 1.89 万亿元，
高于万得一致预期的 3.13 万亿元。9 月社融存量同比增长 7.1%，较 8 月下降了
0.07 个百分点，基本持平于万得一致预期的 7.06%。9 月社融口径新增人民币贷
款 1.27 万亿元，较去年同期少增 3342 亿元，较 8 月多增 1.22 万亿元。 
 9 月新增社融较去年同期小幅多增，主要原因是政府债券较去年同期多增了 3438
亿元，同时委托贷款和外币贷款也较去年同期有所多增；从当月新增规模看，新
增融资规模较大的项目，主要是政府债券、人民币贷款和汇票，当月少增的是信
托贷款和企业债。 值得关注的是9 月股票融资表现依然较好， 当月新增658 亿元，
较去年同期也有小幅上升， 但企业债少增425 亿元， 表明企业融资需求依然不强。 
 从社融存量结构来看，2026 年 9 月与 8 月相比，占比上升的有政府债券（上升
0.17 个百分点）和汇票（上升 0.04 个百分点），占比下降的有股票融资（下降
0.01 个百分点）、信托贷款（下降 0.02 个百分点）、企业债（下降 0.08 个百分
点）和人民币贷款（下降 0.16 个百分点）。与去年同期相比，9 月占比上升较多
的是政府债券（上升 1.30 个百分点）和企业债（上升 0.16 个百分点），占比下
降较多的是人民币贷款 （下降1.31 个百分点） 和委托贷款 （下降0.13 个百分点） 。
虽然新增企业债融资规模少增，但企业债融资占比仍有上升趋势。 
 9 月 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较 8 月下降 0.3 个百分点；M1 同比增长 3.2%，较 8 月
下降 0.9 个百分点；M0 同比增长 10.6%，较 8 月下降 0.6 个百分点。9 月活期存
款（M1-M0）环比上升 0.79%，定期存款（M2-M1）环比上升 0.76%，与去年同
期相比，活期存款环比增速偏低，定期存款环比增速偏高，关注资本市场指数波
动对存款变化趋势的影响。 
 9 月关注非银存款回流。9 月金融机构新增存款 1.77 万亿元，多增的主要是居民
存款 2.40 万亿元和企业存款 1.31 万亿元，财政存款和非银存款均少增；与去年
同期相比，9 月新增存款少增 4400 亿元，其中非银存款少增 8500 亿元，居民存
款少增 5600 亿元。9 月新增存款不弱， 但与去年同期相较不强， 其中值得关注的
细节较多：一是企业存款多增，一定程度上印证了企业融资需求不强的原因，二
是居民存款较去年同期少增，关注居民收入增速变化情况，三是财政存款较去年
同期多增， 关注财政支出是否偏慢， 以及对固投增速的影响， 四是非银存款偏弱，
可能代表投资者对资本市场的态度短期偏谨慎。 
 9 月实体经济融资需求延续偏弱。9 月金融机构口径新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿元，
略高于万得一致预期的 9880 亿元。 其中， 新增短贷及票据3200 亿元， 中长贷9774
亿元， 企业贷1.29 万亿元。 我们认为有三处值得关注， 一是虽然新增贷款超预期，
但是较去年同期少增，主要是居民贷款少增较多，二是新增企业贷款较多，与企
业债发行融资需求偏弱有所不同，三是居民短贷和企业短贷均较去年同期少增较
多。我们认为贷款数据指向了居民部门加杠杆需求不强，以及企业部门内部出现
分化，其中信用等级较高的企业融资需求不强，但依靠信贷的企业中长期贷款需
求仍在。 
 关注外部环境对适度宽松货币政策的影响。9 月底以来，央行货币政策出台了三
项主要调整，一是央行等八部门联合印发《关于金融支持服务业扩能提质的指导
意见》，二是 9 月 29 日发布调整完善若干货币政策工具，包括下调抵押补充贷
款（PSL）利率 0.25 个百分点，将“六张网”建设纳入抵押补充贷款支持领域，及
再贷款扩容等，三是强化财政金融政策协同，实施居民购房贷款贴息政策。整体
来看适度宽松的货币政策仍在发力，但货币政策继续宽松的约束也在加强：从国
内方面看，9 月企业新发放贷款加权平均利率略低于 3%，比上年同期低约 0.2 个
百分点，个人住房新发放贷款加权平均利率约为 3.1%，与上年同期基本持平，9
月底银行间隔夜拆借利率在 1.36%的较低水平，十年期国债利率在 1.68%的较低
水平，人民币对美元汇率增长 10 月初升值突破 6.7；从海外看，市场方面，9 月
底十年美债收益率达到 5.29%的较高水平，同时美联储已于 9 月落地一次加息，
并且纪要显示年内仍将有一次加息。我们认为下一阶段需要关注“适度宽松”的
货币政策可能受外部环境影响，出现国内金融条件“被动收缩”的情况。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济回落速度偏快；国际局势复杂化。

本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。  
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况 。 如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的 ， 请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。  
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

## Document 164: AI算力战进入下半场：从拼单芯XPU性能到拼系统，电力、散热和网络成新战场

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI算力战进入下半场：从拼单芯XPU性能到拼系统，电力、散热和网络成新战场.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 基础设施竞争逻辑正在重构：美银证券跟踪 OCP 峰会后指出，算⼒基本单元已从单颗芯
⽚升级为整机架系统，电⼒、散热与⽹络互联取代芯⽚性能成为核⼼竞争变量。 AMD 披露
两年内 AI token 消耗⽉增 95 倍，英伟达将设计指标锚定 token 吞吐量与成本效率。 AI 资本开
⽀正从产品周期演变为完整基础设施建设⻓周期。
摘要：
AI 基础设施竞争的逻辑正在发⽣根本性转变。
据追⻛交易台，美银证券研究团队在跟踪 OCP 全球峰会后发布报告，AI 算⼒的基本计算单元已
从单颗芯⽚升级为整机架系统，电⼒供给、散热能⼒与⽹络互联正取代芯⽚性能本⾝，成为决
定 AI 基础设施竞争⼒的核⼼变量。
AMD ⾸席执⾏官 Lisa Su 在峰会上披露，过去两年 AI token 消耗量⽉度增幅⾼达 95 倍。英伟达则
已将系统设计框架从芯⽚算⼒转向 token 吞吐量、每⽡ token 数及每 token 成本。这⼀表述上的转
变，折射出整个⾏业竞争维度的深层迁移。美银证券指出， AI 资本开⽀正从单⼀产品周期演变为

完整的基础设施建设周期。
峰会讨论显⽰， AI 基础设施需求并⽆放缓迹象。 OCP 正为 1 兆⽡（ MW ）级机架、吉⽡（ GW ）
级数据中⼼乃⾄多 GW 园区做技术储备。 Broadcom ⾸席执⾏官 Hock Tan 此前表⽰，资本是当前
AI 扩张最⼤的制约因素，但峰会现场的技术路线图讨论并未因此收缩。
机架成为新计算单元，系统级优化取代芯⽚级竞争
美银证券研究团队出席了于圣何塞举⾏的 OCP 全球峰会 2026 ，并在报告中归纳出四条贯穿全天
主旨演讲与技术分论坛的核⼼主题：机架级系统正成为 AI 基础设施的新计算单元； AI token 需求
持续指数级增⻓，驱动算⼒与存储需求同步扩张；光互联与以太⽹架构正从传统的横向扩展
（ scale-out ）延伸⾄纵向扩展（ scale-up ）场景； 800V 直流供电、基于 CXL 与 HBF 的内存解聚
（⽤于 KV 缓存），以及开放⽣态与控制框架，正成为下⼀代 AI ⼯⼚的关键使能技术。
报告强调，⾏业对话的重⼼正从单颗芯⽚性能，转向对涵盖算⼒、内存、⽹络、电⼒与软件编

排的整体 AI 系统的协同优化。能否⾼效扩展 AI 基础设施，正在成为企业间最核⼼的竞争差异所
在。
光互联全⾯向 scale-up 渗透， CRDO 短距光学竞争⼒凸显
⽹络与互联是本届 OCP 峰会最具持续性的结构性主题之⼀。 Broadcom 持续倡导以以太⽹作为横
跨 scale-up 、 scale-out 与 scale-across 三类 AI 架构的统⼀互联底座，多家⼚商亦着重强调机架内
部及机架间光学内容的持续增加。
峰会讨论的焦点之⼀，是 scale-up 互联从铜缆向近封装光学（ NPO ）、并最终迈向共封装光学
（ CPO ）的技术演进路径。 CRDO 表⽰，其短距光学产品（ ZF ALC ）在可靠性、功耗及系统总
成本⽅⾯，对铜缆的竞争⼒正持续提升。
美银证券指出，市场对 Credo Technology （ CRDO ）在 scale-up 光学领域的机会存在低估 —— 该
⾏预计 CRDO 在 FY27 年度光学业务总规模将超过 6 亿美元， 6.4T scale-up NPO 产品将于 FY28 年

度进⼊放量阶段。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

800V 直流供电架构崛起，电⼒与散热成基础设施新瓶颈
电⼒是峰会另⼀核⼼议题。 Delta 与英伟达在会上共同阐述了向 800V 直流（ 800VDC ）架构迁移
的技术路径 —— 随着机架功率密度从当前约 145 千⽡向 300 ⾄ 500 千⽡以上演进，新⼀代供电架
构旨在提升电⼒转换效率、降低转换损耗，并加速 GW 级 AI ⼯⼚的部署节奏。
OCP 各分论坛的讨论共同印证了⼀个判断：未来 AI 基础设施的扩张瓶颈，将越来越多地落在电
⼒可⽤性、散热能⼒与直流容量上，⽽⾮单纯的硅芯⽚供给。这⼀结构性约束⽀撑了横跨电
⼒、散热、⽹络与算⼒的多年期基础设施建设周期，⽽⾮仅围绕单⼀芯⽚产品的短期采购周
期。
需求能⻅度持续， AI 资本开⽀进⼊⻓周期
尽管投资者围绕 AI 投资回报率、基础设施融资模式及 AI 资本开⽀⻓期可持续性的争论持续存在，
本届 OCP 峰会的讨论并未呈现出需求放缓的信号。
AMD 的 Lisa Su 以过去两年 token 消耗量⽉度增⻓ 95 倍的数据，直观呈现了 AI 推理需求的爆发态
势。英伟达则将系统设计的核⼼指标锚定于 token 吞吐量与成本效率，⽽⾮单纯的算⼒规格。
从容量规划路线图来看， OCP 正在为 1MW 级机架、 GW 级设施及多 GW 园区进⾏技术储备， AI 资
本开⽀的周期属性已从单⼀产品迭代升级为完整的基础设施建设浪潮。

## Document 208: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-05)

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

spike feed acyclical inflation.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

A) The Yen Regains Some Strength. B) 5-Year Forward Yields Hold Above 5%.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.
Investment Implications
While some key variables are
complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings
growth, robust consumer demand
(real final sales to private domestic
purchasers grew at 4.6% for Q2) and
ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.
Exhibit 4: U.S. Exceptionalism: Record Inflows of Portfolio and FDI Inflows.
A) Net Foreign Purchases of US Equities and Investment Funds. B) FDI Inflows to the U.S.
-400

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

## Document 211: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

## Document 261: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising

### Page 3

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which
likely represents the OBR's observation window for this budget
Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise

the major taxes, which together account for around 70% of total

Our best guess is that the budget will include a hike in the Bank
Surcharge (raising £2bn if increased from 3% to 8%), together with
a broadening of the tax base for National Insurance to landlords
and partners in LLPs (together yielding around £2bn/year). Other
options, like ending a loophole on stamp duty for commercial
property or lowering the VAT threshold for businesses, have also

been on the table.
The problem with many of these measures is that they require
massive changes to stand any chance of raising material sums,
risking big behavioural reactions while oﬀering uncertain revenue
potential. There’s a reason why former chancellor Rachel Reeves
shied away from many of the measures we outline in the chart
below.

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,

### Page 8

including to the infamous ‘triple lock’, this feels like too much of a
political gamble at this stage.
The negative surprise would be if the Treasury decided to raise the
basic tax allowance. Burnham ﬂirted with this on his ﬁrst day in
oﬃce; it would not only be expensive (potentially £10bn), but it
would also signal that the UK’s commitment to the threshold freeze
– which is doing the lion's share of the planned ﬁscal consolidation
– is waning.
A big rise in the National Living Wage

The minimum wage increase in April 2027, which will be
announced alongside the budget, is perhaps the most important
variable for the Bank of England.
This is based on a recommendation from the Low Pay Commission,
which earlier this year provisionally estimated the increase at 3.7%.
That’s a smaller increase than over the past two years and,
crucially, is not a million miles above the level the Bank considers
consistent with a 2% medium-term inﬂation target (3.25%).

But the LPC has some ﬂexibility in its recommendation, and one
criterion is the cost of living. Higher inﬂation means we could see a
ﬁnal proposal between 4% and 4.5%. Anything above that would,
we suspect, be problematic for the Bank of England and would
bolster the case for higher rates.
The government is no doubt well aware of this. And there appears
to be growing recognition among Labour ﬁgures, including on the
left of the party, that the big minimum wage increases, coupled

with previous payroll tax increases, have had a big impact on
business – particularly in consumer services.
But if the LPC were to recommend minimum wage growth of close
to 5%, could the government resist it? In theory, yes. But politically,
we suspect not.
A surge in bond issuance
We already know that bond issuance is set to increase again in
2027, though for reasons unrelated to the UK’s ﬁscal trajectory.

Still, the point is that, beneath these numbers, public sector net
borrowing is still likely to be projected to keep falling through the
rest of the decade. The major risk – related to some of the things

discussed earlier, like larger near-term spending or surprise
increases in tax allowances – would jeopardise this fundamental
improvement in bond issuance in the medium term. That's
something investors wouldn’t take kindly to.

## Document 279: GMO高管：股票超供和AI基建放缓，将共同引爆这轮AI泡沫

Source PDF: all report pdf/GMO高管：股票超供和AI基建放缓，将共同引爆这轮AI泡沫.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

GMO ⾼管 Inker 警告，史上最⼤规模 IPO 浪潮 ——SpaceX 、 OpenAI 、 Anthropic 合计将向
市场注⼊相当于美股市值 6% ⾄ 7.5% 的新增股票供给，这⼀规模远超互联⽹泡沫⾼峰期的历
史纪录。他指出，若叠加 AI 算⼒从紧缺转向过剩的拐点，市场跌幅可能达到 35% ⾄ 50% 。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 15 ⽇，美国通胀黏性推升加息预期，亚太半导体与存储产业链遭遇全线重挫， AI 算⼒独⻆
兽 CoreWeave 债券跌破⾯值 80% 、五年违约概率突破 50% 。
在这个全球科技资产剧烈动荡的关键节点，管理规模超 800 亿美元的波⼠顿知名资产管理机构
GMO ，由联席资产配置主管本 · 英克（ Ben Inker ）等联名近⽇发布了引发华尔街⾼度关注的三
季度重磅研报 —— 《 AI 泡沫破裂的催化剂》（ A Catalyst for the AI Bubble Break ）。该报告预
⾔：未来 12 ⾄ 18 个⽉内，前所未有的股权供给潮将刺破 AI 泡沫。

随后， Ben Inker 做客播客《 Other People's Money 》，阐述了他在三季度投资信中提出的判断。
他表⽰， SpaceX 、 Anthropic 、 OpenAI 三家公司的 IPO 及后续解禁，将在约 12 个⽉内向市场注
⼊相当于美股总市值 6% ⾄ 7.5% 的新增股票供给。
Inker 进⼀步指出，若 AI 算⼒同步从结构性紧缺滑向产能过剩， “ 我们将看到信贷端的⼤规模违
约，股权端的公司破产，以及超⼤规模云服务商投资回报率惨不忍睹的局⾯。那不是市场下跌
18% ，⽽是下跌 35% 到 50% 。 ”
历史上每⼀次技术⾰命都制造了泡沫
AI 是真实的技术变⾰，但真实的技术变⾰历来是泡沫的温床。 Inker 说：
在⼀般情况下，市场每增加 1% 的 IPO 市值占⽐，当年回报就会差 4 个百分点。如果我们看到 6%
的供给涌⼊，那就是⽐正常年份差 24 个百分点。
运河、铁路、汽⻋、电⽓化、互联⽹ —— 每⼀次变⾰性技术都制造了泡沫。每个⼈都能看出来，
天哪，这将改变世界。于是他们说，我真的想参与其中。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

问题在于，变⾰性技术的受益者极难事先判断，⽽⼤量涌⼊的资本本⾝就会摧毁回报。他以英
国铁路热潮为例：伦敦到曼彻斯特的第⼀条铁路利润丰厚，但到铁路热潮结束时，同⼀条线路
上建了六条铁路，没有⼀条能赚到钱。
落到 AI 的具体商业结构， Inker 认为问题同样清晰：⽆论是算⼒供给还是 AI 模型，都更像竞争性
⾏业，⽽⾮赢家通吃的格局。
他点出这⼀逻辑的商业终点： “ 如果 AI 要被⼴泛使⽤，它就必须便宜。⽽便宜的东西很难同时⼜
利润丰厚。”
史⽆前例的股票供给冲击正在逼近
Inker 的核⼼论点，是市场结构的变化使股票供给的冲击⼒远超历史。
美股过去数⼗年的底层⽜市逻辑，核⼼⽀撑之⼀在于上市公司持续的股票净回购。企业通过回
购不断回收流通筹码，年均供给缩减约 1% ，给⼤盘提供了稳定的被动买盘⽀撑。
眼下这套微观运⾏逻辑正在发⽣质变。 SpaceX 挂牌上市并开启⼤规模解锁周期， Anthropic 与
OpenAI 也加速⾛向公开市场。海量新筹码集中涌⼊市场，存量资⾦⾯临巨⼤的被动调仓压⼒。

投资者如果想要配置这些超级巨⽆霸，势必需要通过⼤量抛售现有持仓来腾挪资⾦，这会直接
压低⼤盘整体的估值中枢。
Inker 引⽤历史规律点出了这⼀⻛险的数学乘数：
这⼀轮潜在的供给洪流，在体量上已经刷新了历次科技狂热的纪录。 Inker 回顾了世纪之交的互
联⽹泡沫时期：
针对 SpaceX 在上市后采取的按批次递进解锁模式，英克认为这种精⼼设计的条款，恰恰反向证
实了发⾏⽅对⼆级市场承接极限的深刻忌惮：
当供给超过需求，不只是新增投资回报糟糕，它会把整个⾏业的存量投资价值⼀并摧毁。
这周 Anthropic 的模型最好，下周 OpenAI 的最好，还有来⾃其他实验室、其他国家的开源模型。
⼈们在切换，甚⾄在不同查询之间切换。现在有整个⾏业的存在，就是帮你在任务级别优化这种
切换。
“ 从历史规律来看，新 IPO 占市场总市值的⽐例每上升 1% ，当年美股的表现就会⽐正常年份落后
⼤约 4% 。如果后续进⼊⼆级市场的新增供给达到市值的 6% ，市场回报率将⽐常态低出整整
24% 。 ”
“在 1999 年和 2000 年，进⼊市场的新增供给占总市值的 2.5% 到 4% 左右。此前历史上还从来没有

哪⼀年出现过超过 4% 的供给增量。如果 OpenAI 和 Anthropic 如期推进 IPO ，加上 SpaceX 后续
的流通盘释放，全美新增股权供给可能达到总市值的 6% ⾄ 7.5% ，这是过去 12 个⽉维度上从未⻅
过的规模。”

### Page 3

算⼒过剩：从 “ 短缺溢价 ” 到 “ 旧资产清零 ”
股票供给冲击是 Inker 判断的第⼀层，算⼒从紧缺转向过剩则是可能将市场推向极端情景的第⼆
层。
他描述了⼀个⾃我强化的过剩逻辑：⽬前⼤量算⼒消耗在 AI 模型训练上。⼀旦市场意识到训练投
⼊过度，训练需求下降，原本被训练占⽤的算⼒就会释放出来，整个市场的算⼒供给将骤然宽
松。
在基础设施端，数据中⼼天然追求标准接⼝与硬件互通，业内甚⾄开始尝试将算⼒打包成标准
化的期货合约。⼀旦算⼒蜕变为⼤宗商品，科技巨头便⽆法单靠出租算⼒来获得持续的超⾼资
本回报。
眼下 CoreWeave 债券跌破 80 美分、亚洲存储芯⽚⻰头集体⼤跌，正是资本市场开始对 AI 算⼒现
⾦流产⽣信任危机的直接表现。
“SpaceX 管理层很清楚冲击有多⼤。即便采取分期解锁，也改变不了总量的现实。在接下来的 12
个⽉⾥，⼤约价值 2 万亿美元的 SpaceX 股票将陆续具备出售条件。持有者必然会卖掉很⼤⼀部
分，⽽场内买家只能靠抛售⼿头的其他股票来接盘。这对整个⼆级市场的流动性构成了沉重考

验。 ”
“ 现在的算⼒⽀出带有极强的内耗性质。⼤约⼀半的算⼒完全耗费在训练新模型上。如果⾏业突
然达成共识，发现⼤家在模型训练上烧了太多冤枉钱，把训练算⼒占⽐从 50% ⼀路削减到 10% ，
市场上瞬间就会出现海量被闲置出来的可⽤算⼒。 ”
如果我们都缺算⼒，那我当然愿意为⼀块五年前的 GPU 付钱，哪怕它能效更低。但如果世界突
然算⼒泛滥，那块五年前的数据中⼼还不如直接关掉 —— 因为我⽤最新的数据中⼼⽣成⼀个
token 消耗的电更少，⽽且机房容量完全管够，客⼾没有理由再为空余的⽼旧硬件付费。

### Page 4

Inker 指出，供求失衡绝不会仅仅停留在产业层⾯，它会⽴刻沿着资⾦链条引爆⾦融市场的系统
性震荡：
他特别区分了不同公司的脆弱程度。微软的数据中⼼投资回报率可能令⼈失望，但微软不会违
约。 “Coreweave 不会那么幸运。 Super Micro 可能也不会。 ” 他说：
他还指出，泡沫破裂往往先于基本⾯恶化被证实。
信贷市场：过度集中的隐患已在积聚
除股票市场外， Inker 对信贷市场同样表达了担忧，尽管他认为⽬前尚未出现系统性危机的明确
信号。
他指出，当前⾼收益债和投资级债券的利差均处于历史低位，约为历史第 13 百分位。与此同
时， AI 相关债务正在信贷市场中形成⾼度集中的敞⼝。
他还注意到，评级较低的三 C 级债券利差已经⾛阔⾄历史第 74 百分位，与⾼评级垃圾债的分化明
显。 GMO 信贷团队正在⼩幅介⼊，但尚未形成⼤规模配置，原因在于许多⾼⻛险债务结构建⽴
于 2020 ⾄ 2021 年零利率环境下，在当前 5% 利率⽔平下已⽆法正常运转，需要重组。
对于政府能否充当最后的救助者， Inker 持保留态度。

“ 如果这种局⾯发⽣，信贷市场将⾯临⼤⾯积违约，纯 AI 概念的上市企业会直接⾛向破产，超⼤
规模云⼚商的投资回报率也将变得⾮常糟糕。这种情况下的市场跌幅绝对不会只有 18% ，⽽是可
能演变为 35% 到 50% 的深度崩盘。 ”
这不是说⼀定会发⽣，但如果发⽣了，我们真的不应该感到惊讶 —— 因为这是每⼀次真正⾰命性
技术变⾰都曾经历过的模式。铁路改变了世界，汽⻋改变了世界，互联⽹改变了世界，纳斯达克
跌了⾄少 80% 。
在互联⽹泡沫中，泡沫破裂时，互联⽹流量并没有真的每三个⽉翻四倍这件事还没有被证实。是
其他因素先把它拖下来的。这⾥也很容易发⽣同样的事情。
如果所有计划中的投资都如期落地，这将成为⼀个深刻的问题 —— 企业债中 AI 相关敞⼝的集中
度，将超过现代信贷市场历史上的任何时期。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
230 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
不过他认为，美联储仍有降息空间，且 AI 相关损失⽬前主要集中在股权端⽽⾮银⾏体系，这与互
联⽹泡沫破裂时的情况类似 ——“ 那次损失了很多钱，但没有动摇太多东西，因为损失不在⾼杠
杆机构⼿⾥，不在银⾏⼿⾥。只要损失在正确的地⽅，就可以有重⼤损失⽽不发⽣灾难。 ”
⾮ AI 资产：寻找 “ 有 AI 加成但不是 AI 押注 ” 的标的
⾯对上述⻛险， Inker 的配置逻辑是寻找那些本⾝具备合理估值和回报，同时能从 AI 需求中获得
额外收益的公司，⽽⾮将整个投资组合押注于 AI 本⾝。
他以⽇本公司 Toto 为例： GMO 的⽇本股票团队持有 Toto ，最初看中的是其卫浴业务，后来发现
Toto 凭借对陶瓷材料的深度积累，还有⼀块⽤于半导体传输的陶瓷部件业务，意外成为 AI 供应链

受益者。
在更宏观的配置层⾯， Inker 表⽰ GMO 偏好⾮美股票⽽⾮美股，偏好价值股⽽⾮成⻓股，并⻓期
超配资源类股票和⾼质量股票。他的理由是：资源股与其他资产的相关性随时间拉⻓⽽持续下
降，在通胀冲击情景下具有独特的对冲价值；⾼质量股票则在承担较低经济⻛险的同时，并不
需要以更低的⻓期回报作为代价。
对于韩国、中国台湾等 AI 受益市场，他的态度相对温和。
我不认为政府还负担得起充当救助者的⻆⾊。我们有⼤量债务，⾚字规模很⼤，我不知道钱从哪
⾥来，⽽且如果你动摇了国债市场，你造成的损害可能⽐你能提供的帮助更⼤。
我们寻找的是如何构建⼀个不只是 AI 押注、但有 AI 加成的投资组合。
这些公司利润涨了 10 倍，股价涨了 7 倍。我们仍然持有，因为利润增速快于股价涨幅，但我们减
少了⼀些，因为我们不认为这些利润完全可持续。

SUMMARY: AI euphoria meets macro headwinds
Tech remained a major driver of equity market performance in Q3. The
Mag 7 outperformed the broader market while the S&P 500 also

posted gains, lifted by mega-cap and tech stocks.
Still, tech’s path in Q3 was volatile. July began with a reversal of the AI
trade as investors questioned elevated valuations, the sustainability of
AI spending and the outlook for semiconductor demand. That led to a
large sell-off in chip stocks, with large falls in Q2 outperformers SK
Hynix and Samsung contributing to a sharp decline in Korea’s KOSPI.
The Philly SOX experienced its worst monthly performance since

2008.
By August, however, sentiment had improved. The S&P 500 reached
fresh record highs, even as US Treasury yields rose, supported by
stronger cloud earnings and signs of AI monetization. September
proved more challenging as higher oil prices, inflation concerns and
rising bond yields weighed on risk assets, particularly semiconductors.
The Fed also raised its policy rate by 25bp on Sep 16.
Yet, despite these macro headwinds, company guidance continued to

indicate strong AI infrastructure demand, with Micron’s stronger-than-
expected revenue guidance on Sep 30 providing further evidence of
robust demand for memory chips. Memory supply remains tight,
semiconductor capex is accelerating, and the industry’s attention has
increasingly turned to bottlenecks in optical networking and photonics,
as well as concerns around AI regulation and safety. Looking ahead,
tech is likely to remain an important driver of equity returns into Q4,

although elevated valuations and tighter financial conditions leave the
sector sensitive to earnings disappointments. Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.
Q3 started with doubts about the AI trade but ended with renewed confidence

1. GLOBAL INDUSTRY BREAKDOWN
Tech hardware, including Lenovo, Advantech, and HP Inc, rebounded in Q3. This was partly driven by accelerating AI

server demand and the ongoing data center build-out, although continuing memory shortages raised costs for
hardware manufacturers. Cloud and server providers, like Oracle, Dell and HPE, also highlighted strong AI-related
demand. Semis and equipment were the weakest performers. Elsewhere, entertainment and telecoms posted modest
declines. Finally, in Q3 investor attention increasingly shifted to AI agents, with Meta’s Muse, Microsoft’s Copilot and

Salesforce’s Agentforce among the prominent offerings.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Median returns taken. Updated Sep 30.

2. SEMICONDUCTORS
Chip stocks were hurt initially by a reversal of the AI trade and then by rising bond yields. The Philly SOX index fell
sharply in Q3, after its worst monthly performance in July (-21%) since 2008. Concerns about higher oil prices and
rising rates continued to weigh on the sector in Aug and Sep. SK Hynix was among the weakest performers, amid an

earnings disappointment relative to expectations and an unwinding of leveraged positions. Its Nasdaq ADRs began
trading in July. Despite these headwinds, the semis outlook remains supported by AI infrastructure demand. Within
semis, potential long-term beneficiaries include suppliers of AI infrastructure, memory including high-bandwidth
memory (HBM), advanced packaging, photonics and semiconductor equipment.

4. REGIONAL BREAKDOWN
After outperforming in Q2, mainland Chinese tech stocks posted large declines this quarter, hurt by July’s sell-off.
The CSI 300 tech series led the declines. Weakness also extended across Asia more broadly, with the KOSPI, Nikkei 225
and Hang Seng Tech down in Q3, driven by a chip sell-off and concerns over further Fed hikes. European tech equities
were more resilient, with the STOXX Europe 600 Tech Index down slightly lower in Q3 but still well above its year-end

level. Within European tech, Infineon underperformed, while Orange illustrated weakness in telecoms outside the index.

## Document 340: Sizing Up US Debt

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sizing Up US Debt.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Sizing Up US Debt
Date:    10 minutes ago
Sizing Up US Debt

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Sizing Up US Debt
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 12 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by dostonxd / Unsplash

I. Adding It Up
The bond market had a good day today: It was closed for Columbus Day.
We think that yields have risen high enough to attract buyers. If so, then
yields should stabilize around current levels. Plenty could still go wrong,
making bond yields even more attractive. The war in the Middle East is
likely to heat up again after the midterm elections, sending oil prices

### Page 2

higher. On the other hand, this week's core CPI is likely to rise just 0.2%,
which would likely postpone a Fed rate hike from later this month to early
December.
For now, let's focus on the supply of debt in the US, which is a longer-term
concern for the bond market.
The US Treasury borrowed $749 billion during the three months through
September (chart). That's the change in marketable securities held by the
public over the three months. The 10-year Treasury bond yield rose from

roughly 4.50% to 5.25% over that period. In the past, the supply of
government securities rarely seemed to drive bond yields. The key drivers
were expected and actual inflation and the Fed's reaction to both.
In the past, federal budget deficits tended to widen the most during
recessions, when tax revenues fell faster than the government’s outlays
(chart). The supply of Treasury securities would be increased as the Fed
lowered interest rates to revive economic growth. The deficits would

narrow during economic expansions, as revenues rose faster than
outlays.
Over the past three years, the economy has been booming, but the
government’s budget deficit remains very wide. It hasn't narrowed
because outlays are growing faster than receipts. Allowing that to happen
is fiscal recklessness. That’s because it requires increasing the supply of
government debt during an economic boom, when private credit demands
are surging, which necessarily puts upward pressure on interest rates. If

### Page 3

rates get high enough to cause a credit crunch and a recession, attempts
to revive the economy with deficit-bloating fiscal stimulus could backfire by
pushing bond yields higher. If deficits are this large during good times,
what happens when the bad times arrive?
For now, we believe the backup in US bond yields is driven more by
better-than-expected US economic growth than by the government's
deficit. In our view, bond yields are back to their "old normal," i.e.,

consistent with robust economic growth. In other words, the US isn't on
the edge of a debt crisis. In the Roaring 2020s scenario we expect,
productivity-led economic growth boosts government revenues and lowers
America's deficit and debt ratios relative to nominal GDP. Both Treasury
Secretary Scott Bessent and Fed Chair Kevin Warsh believe this will
happen. The question is whether the Bond Vigilantes will patiently wait for
this outcome. We're not so sure.

For perspective, let's add up US debt and compare it to public and private
debt in other countries:
(1) Total US debt reached a record $118.9 trillion in Q2, including $83.8
trillion in nonfinancial debt and $27.7 trillion in financial debt (chart).

### Page 4

(2) The composition of nonfinancial debt has shifted dramatically since
the Great Financial Crisis (GFC). Before 2008, Treasury debt was smaller
than household and business debt. That relationship reversed as
Washington borrowed heavily during the GFC while the private sector
deleveraged. The Great Virus Crisis (GVC) in 2020 accelerated the shift.
By Q2-2026, marketable Treasury debt held by the public had reached a
record $30.9 trillion, exceeding both household and business debt (chart).

(3) Relative to nominal GDP, total US debt has declined since the GVC
to 374.1% in Q2 (chart). The financial sector drove much of the decline,
with financial debt falling from around 150% to 87.0% of GDP since the
GFC. Nonfinancial debt is at 263.8%, remaining relatively stable in recent
years despite rising government debt, with a brief exception during the
GVC.

### Page 5

That stability masks a significant shift. Treasury debt has surged from
around 30% of GDP before the GFC to 97.2% in Q2, while household
debt has fallen from nearly 100% to 67.0% and business debt has also
declined (chart). Private-sector deleveraging has partly offset rising
government indebtedness relative to GDP.
Separating Treasury debt from total nonfinancial debt makes the
divergence even clearer. Nonfinancial debt excluding Treasuries has fallen

from roughly 200% of GDP during the GFC to 166.5% in Q2, as Treasury
debt surged to 97.2% of GDP (chart).

### Page 6

(4) Total US debt securities and loans reached a record $120.1 trillion in
Q2, comprising $67.3 trillion in securities and $52.7 trillion in loans (chart).
Securities have grown faster than loans since the GFC.
Marketable Treasury securities held by the public have surged to $30.9
trillion, far exceeding corporate and foreign bonds, which they overtook
following the GFC (chart). Government borrowing has become
increasingly dominant in US debt markets.

### Page 7

In short, private-sector debt is down relative to GDP since the GFC, while
the public sector has become more indebted relative to GDP. Healthier
household and business balance sheets should make the economy more
resilient to future shocks, reducing the potential need for government
bailouts and additional borrowing. That could help preserve Treasuries'
traditional role as a safe haven during the next crisis, despite elevated
government debt.
II. Global Comparisons

(1) Total debt/GDP. Japan leads the pack, followed by France, Canada,
and China. The US is roughly in line with the developed country average,
while Germany has the lowest debt ratio among the countries shown
(chart).

### Page 8

(2) Government debt/GDP. Japan also tops the government debt ranking,
followed by Italy and China. The US and France are well above the
developed country average, while Germany stands out with a relatively
low government debt burden (chart).
(3) Private debt/GDP. Canada, France, and China have the highest
private-sector debt ratios, while the US ranks below the developed country
average. Italy has the lowest private-sector debt ratio among the countries

shown (chart).
💡
Join the discussion with Dr. Ed below! Paid members can now
leave comments or questions at the end of our QuickTakes, and it
may be featured in the next "Just Ask Dr. Ed!"

### Page 9

Our advice to investors
評估  Intel Foundry 轉型價值：投資⼈可能要分拆評估  Intel 的晶圓代⼯與
封裝代⼯業務。 EMIB/Foveros 獲外部頂級客戶認證代表先進封裝⽑利率具備
修復空間，但  Intel 晶圓製造良率（ 18A/14A ）仍具執⾏⾵險。台積電前段
2nm 與  A16 先進製程市占率依然⾼達  90% 以上，封裝委外反⽽可使台積電
釋放 CoWoS 產能，專注於⽑利率⾼達  65%+ 的前段晶圓代⼯，短期結構性影
響不⼤。
Non consensus outside the box thinking
市場普遍將「 Nvidia 採⽤  Foveros 」解讀為  Intel 晶圓代⼯成功扳倒台積
電，但本質上是 AI 加速器  GPU/TPU/ASIC 由於⾯積持續擴⼤，突破光罩極限

### Page 3

考，並⾮投資建議，個⼈應審慎評估⾵險，如因此造成任何損失，本⼈不負任

聯友金屬 7610 TT (綠能環保) 
供應跟不上需求擴大 
投資與評價 
1. 3Q26 受惠國際鎢供應持續吃緊、鎢價維持高檔，加上屏東二廠新增
30%產能陸續開出，自售比重持續提升，預估營收季增 47.5%，毛利率
66.5%，EPS 27.32 元創單季新高。 
2. 2027 年成長動能由鎢價轉向擴產與產品高值化，屏南三廠、屏科廠陸
續開出，產品由鎢酸鈉延伸至 APT、氧化鎢、鎢粉及碳化鎢，自售比重
有機會挑戰 60%，預估 2026/27 EPS 83.77/109.27 元。 
3. 目前股價評價位於近兩年本益比 10～24 倍區間中段，考量非中鎢供應
鏈稀缺性、自售比重提升及 2027 年新產能接棒，給予區間中間偏上的
22 倍 FY27E PER，給予強力買進評等，目標價 2,400 元(22x FY27E 
EPS)。

重點更新 
3Q26 鎢價與自售業務推升獲利續增 
預估 3Q26 營收 28.43 億元（+47.5% QoQ） ，主要受惠國際鎢價維持高檔、
鎢酸鈉自售業務成長，以及屏東二廠新增 30%產能陸續開出；毛利 18.90 億
元（+44.8% QoQ） ，毛利率66.5%（-1.3ppts QoQ） ，營收與毛利仍維持高
速成長 。 營業費用1.88 億元 （+55.6% QoQ） ， 稅後淨利13.27 億元 （+40.3% 
QoQ），EPS 27.32 元創單季新高。預估 4Q26 隨新產能進一步量產、自售比
重過半，營收29.59 億元 （+4.1% QoQ） ，惟費用投入增加，EPS 降至 26.17
元。 
2027 年產能與高值化產品接棒 
預估 2027 年營收 132.79 億元 （+52.1% YoY） ，成長動能由鎢價逐步轉向產
能擴張及產品高值化，屏南三廠與屏科廠新產能陸續開出，產品由鎢酸鈉延
伸至 APT、氧化鎢、鎢粉及碳化鎢，自售比重有機會挑戰 60%；毛利 87.22
億元（+48.9% YoY） ，毛利率65.7%（-1.4ppts YoY） 。惟新廠與新工序投入
增加 ， 營業費用20.62 億元 （+202.8% YoY） ， 使營業利益66.60 億元 （+28.7% 
YoY） 、 營益率50.2%（-9.1ppts YoY） ； 稅後淨利53.09 億元 （+30.4% YoY），
EPS 109.27 元再創年度新高。後續營運重點將轉向新產能利用率，以及高值
化產品的提升速率。
